# Recovery Workbench — AI engineering standards (v2)

This is the standards file the coding agent reads before every task, as defined in
`Recovery Workbench AI Assisted Development Approach v1` (section 3). It applies to the recovery
module of the ACE service and to anyone using an AI tool on this repository.

**Owner:** Solution architect with the TechX tech leads.
**Change control:** pull request, reviewed like code.
**Baseline:** document set v14, 16 November 2026. The implementation evidence from 10 November 2026
(`RWB-E1-042`, `RWB-TE-001`) is preserved; both are re-specified against this baseline.
**Source:** `requirement/legacy/08-ai-and-agents/recovery-workbench-ai-assisted-development-approach-v1.md`
and `requirement/legacy/11-delivery-and-operations/recovery-workbench-engineering-practices-v5-docx.md`.

## 1. The seven working rules

1. The person who merges a change owns it, whoever or whatever wrote it.
2. The agent works from files: the standards file, the story file and the plan file. Anything the
   agent needs is in one of the three.
3. The tests and the acceptance criteria exist before the code is generated.
4. Every line that reaches production is read by a person.
5. An AI error found by a reviewer is written into this file, so the agent is told not to repeat it.
6. No personal data or production data goes into a prompt, a test or a log.
7. The unit of work is one story. Work is merged at least once a working day.

## 2. Structure and stack

- The Workbench is the recovery module of the ACE service: one service, one repository, one front end.
- Back end: Spring Boot (Java 21), Spring JPA, Spring Batch, Spring Security with method security,
  Spring Session, Spring Cloud Azure, Spring Data REST, Spring HATEOAS and Quartz.
- Front end: React on Vite with Radix and TanStack Query. Front-end types are generated from the
  back end.
- Database: PostgreSQL, schema `recovery` in the ACE database, referenced only as
  `${recoverySchema}` and fixed to `recovery` in every environment (D-RCV-28). Schema changes use
  versioned migrations, one migration per story, forward-only. Files are named
  `V<seq>__<story>_<name>.sql`, where `<seq>` is a four-digit sequence taken in merge order and line 1
  is `-- Story: <story key>`.
- Domain driven design, RESTful API, end-to-end type safety and validation.
- Cases are modelled in CMMN 1.1 terms: case, stage, milestone, task, sentry, event listener and
  case file item. From Epic 3 the case lifecycle runs on Temporal; case data stays in the recovery
  schema (D-RCV-36, ADR-011).
- Rules are versioned data, authored as Drools XLS decision-table packages and evaluated by embedded
  Drools (D-RCV-36, ADR-012).
- Hosting: the ACE namespace on the shared ACE AKS clusters; Temporal is self-hosted in the same
  namespace.

## 3. The agent must

1. Put recovery code in the recovery module folder only. Change the common or collection module only
   where the story file names it.
2. Read every threshold, limit, timer, queue, role, template and route from its versioned reference
   table. Never write one as a literal in code.
3. Put a Spring Security method check on every endpoint, naming the capability area and the operation.
4. Write a decision record entry for every change to a case and every decision, with the rule ID and
   version used.
5. Use the names in the business glossary for classes, fields, tables and endpoints.
6. Return each validation message from the message table by its rule ID.
7. Make database changes by migration script only, one script per story. Name it
   `V<seq>__<story>_<name>.sql`; line 1 is `-- Story: <story key>`. A corrective script takes the
   next sequence, keeps the original story key and adds line 2 `-- Fix: <reason>` (D-RCV-28).
8. Use Spring Batch and Quartz for every Epic 1...

Instructions from: C:\Users\SahilMore\Downloads\Recovery Workbench SharePoint Set v14\rwb-wt\RWB-TE-001\AGENTS.md
# Recovery Workbench — AI engineering standards (v2)

This is the standards file the coding agent reads before every task, as defined in
`Recovery Workbench AI Assisted Development Approach v1` (section 3). It applies to the recovery
module of the ACE service and to anyone using an AI tool on this repository.

**Owner:** Solution architect with the TechX tech leads.
**Change control:** pull request, reviewed like code.
**Baseline:** document set v14, 16 November 2026. The implementation evidence from 10 November 2026
(`RWB-E1-042`, `RWB-TE-001`) is preserved; both are re-specified against this baseline.
**Source:** `requirement/legacy/08-ai-and-agents/recovery-workbench-ai-assisted-development-approach-v1.md`
and `requirement/legacy/11-delivery-and-operations/recovery-workbench-engineering-practices-v5-docx.md`.

## 1. The seven working rules

1. The person who merges a change owns it, whoever or whatever wrote it.
2. The agent works from files: the standards file, the story file and the plan file. Anything the
   agent needs is in one of the three.
3. The tests and the acceptance criteria exist before the code is generated.
4. Every line that reaches production is read by a person.
5. An AI error found by a reviewer is written into this file, so the agent is told not to repeat it.
6. No personal data or production data goes into a prompt, a test or a log.
7. The unit of work is one story. Work is merged at least once a working day.

## 2. Structure and stack

- The Workbench is the recovery module of the ACE service: one service, one repository, one front end.
- Back end: Spring Boot (Java 21), Spring JPA, Spring Batch, Spring Security with method security,
  Spring Session, Spring Cloud Azure, Spring Data REST, Spring HATEOAS and Quartz.
- Front end: React on Vite with Radix and TanStack Query. Front-end types are generated from the
  back end.
- Database: PostgreSQL, schema `recovery` in the ACE database, referenced only as
  `${recoverySchema}` and fixed to `recovery` in every environment (D-RCV-28). Schema changes use
  versioned migrations, one migration per story, forward-only. Files are named
  `V<seq>__<story>_<name>.sql`, where `<seq>` is a four-digit sequence taken in merge order and line 1
  is `-- Story: <story key>`.
- Domain driven design, RESTful API, end-to-end type safety and validation.
- Cases are modelled in CMMN 1.1 terms: case, stage, milestone, task, sentry, event listener and
  case file item. From Epic 3 the case lifecycle runs on Temporal; case data stays in the recovery
  schema (D-RCV-36, ADR-011).
- Rules are versioned data, authored as Drools XLS decision-table packages and evaluated by embedded
  Drools (D-RCV-36, ADR-012).
- Hosting: the ACE namespace on the shared ACE AKS clusters; Temporal is self-hosted in the same
  namespace.

## 3. The agent must

1. Put recovery code in the recovery module folder only. Change the common or collection module only
   where the story file names it.
2. Read every threshold, limit, timer, queue, role, template and route from its versioned reference
   table. Never write one as a literal in code.
3. Put a Spring Security method check on every endpoint, naming the capability area and the operation.
4. Write a decision record entry for every change to a case and every decision, with the rule ID and
   version used.
5. Use the names in the business glossary for classes, fields, tables and endpoints.
6. Return each validation message from the message table by its rule ID.
7. Make database changes by migration script only, one script per story. Name it
   `V<seq>__<story>_<name>.sql`; line 1 is `-- Story: <story key>`. A corrective script takes the
   next sequence, keeps the original story key and adds line 2 `-- Fix: <reason>` (D-RCV-28).
8. Use Spring Batch and Quartz for every Epic 1 and Epi...

```text
# Skill: ponytail

# Ponytail

You are a lazy senior developer. The best code is the code never written. You solve the whole problem with the least new code. End your reply with one or two lines: what you skipped or did not check, and any risk the user must know.

Active for the whole session until the user says "stop ponytail" or "normal mode". Switch level: `/ponytail lite|full|ultra`.

## Before you write

Read the task and the code it touches. List every place your change must reach: callers, tests, fixtures, config, exports. Check what your change could break for users: data it would destroy or expose, callers that stop working. That is scope. Extra features are not.

## The smallest complete change

Take the first option that fully works:

1. Does it need to exist? Skip features, options and flexibility nobody asked for, and name them in one line. A vague request ("build me X") gets the smallest version that does the core job.
2. Already in this codebase (a helper, component, service, pattern)? Use it the way the surrounding code does.
3. Standard library or a platform feature? Use it, unless the project has its own. A house component beats a native widget.
4. An installed dependency? Use it. Never add a dependency for a few lines.
5. Can it be one line a reader gets at a glance? One line.
6. Otherwise: the minimum code that works.

- Be lazy about the solution, never about the change itself: finish every part the task needs, including the callers, tests and fixtures your change breaks.
- No abstraction, wrapper, type conversion, option, config, boilerplate or "for later" code nobody asked for. Keep values in the form the platform already gives you. Deletion beats addition. Keep the structure the codebase already has: its layers, interfaces and conventions.
- The shortest working diff wins, once you know everything it must touch. A one-liner that needs decoding is not short.
- Comment only the why the code cannot show, in one line.
- Bug fix: before you edit, grep every caller of the function you touch, then fix the root cause once in the shared code.
- Code you move or merge keeps its error handling and validation.
- Between options of equal size, take the one that is correct on edge cases.
- Lazy code without its check is unfinished: new non-trivial logic (a branch, a loop, a parser, money or security, or a whole new script or app) leaves one small test or an assert-based self-check. Trivial changes need none.
```

```text
package th.co.scb.ace.demo.recovery.platform.internal;

import java.time.Instant;
import java.util.Date;
import java.util.UUID;

import org.quartz.Job;
import org.quartz.JobBuilder;
import org.quartz.JobDataMap;
import org.quartz.JobDetail;
import org.quartz.JobExecutionContext;
import org.quartz.JobKey;
import org.quartz.Scheduler;
import org.quartz.SchedulerException;
import org.quartz.Trigger;
import org.quartz.TriggerBuilder;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.context.ApplicationEventPublisher;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;

import th.co.scb.ace.demo.recovery.platform.api.TimerScheduler;

/**
 * Epic 1-2 implementation of {@link TimerScheduler} on Quartz (BS-5.1, BS-5.2). The timer row in
 * {@code recovery.timer} stays the record; Quartz only fires it.
 *
 * <p>Locally Quartz uses its in-memory store. In ACE it uses the ACE JDBC job store.
 */
@Component
class QuartzTimerScheduler implements TimerScheduler {

	static final String GROUP = "rwb-timer";

	private final Scheduler scheduler;

	QuartzTimerScheduler(Scheduler scheduler) {
		this.scheduler = scheduler;
	}

	@Override
	public void schedule(UUID timerId, UUID caseId, String timerCode, Instant dueAt) {
		JobKey key = JobKey.jobKey(timerId.toString(), GROUP);
		JobDataMap data = new JobDataMap();
		data.put("timerId", timerId.toString());
		data.put("caseId", caseId.toString());
		data.put("timerCode", timerCode);
		data.put("dueAt", dueAt.toString());
		JobDetail job = JobBuilder.newJob(TimerDueJob.class).withIdentity(key).usingJobData(data).build();
		Trigger trigger = TriggerBuilder.newTrigger().withIdentity(timerId.toString(), GROUP)
				.startAt(Date.from(dueAt)).build();
		try {
			scheduler.scheduleJob(job, java.util.Set.of(trigger), true);
		}
		catch (SchedulerException ex) {
			throw new IllegalStateException("Timer could not be scheduled: " + timerId, ex);
		}
	}

	@Override
	public boolean cancel(UUID timerId) {
		try {
			return scheduler.deleteJob(JobKey.jobKey(timerId.toString(), GROUP));
		}
		catch (SchedulerException ex) {
			throw new IllegalStateException("Timer could not be cancelled: " + timerId, ex);
		}
	}
```

```text
package th.co.scb.ace.demo.recovery.rules.api;

import java.util.List;

/**
 * Embedded Drools evaluator (ADR-012). One evaluator serves the engines, the scenarios, the dry
 * run and the build gate, so a scenario that passes proves the production path.
 *
 * <p>From Epic 3, {@link #evaluate} is called only inside a Temporal activity. The caller writes
 * {@link RuleResult#ruleId()} and {@link RuleResult#packageVersion()} to the decision log.
 */
public interface RuleEngine {

	/**
	 * Compiles a Drools XLS/XLSX decision-table workbook. Does not store or activate it; storing a
	 * {@code rule_package_version} row and RW-RISK approval belong to the rule package story (E1-073).
	 *
	 * @throws RuleCompilationException when the workbook does not compile
	 */
	CompiledRulePackage compile(String packageCode, String versionLabel, String fileName, byte[] workbook);

	/**
	 * Evaluates one fact against a compiled package version. First match: exactly one row decides,
	 * or the result is {@link RuleResult#noMatch}.
	 */
	RuleResult evaluate(String packageCode, String versionLabel, RuleInput input);

	/** Thrown when a workbook does not compile. {@code messages} are Drools builder messages. */
	class RuleCompilationException extends RuntimeException {

		private final List<String> messages;

		public RuleCompilationException(String packageCode, List<String> messages) {
			super("Rule package " + packageCode + " does not compile: " + String.join("; ", messages));
			this.messages = List.copyOf(messages);
		}

		public List<String> messages() {
			return messages;
		}
	}
}
```

```text
package th.co.scb.ace.demo.recovery.workflow.internal;

import java.time.Duration;

import io.temporal.activity.ActivityInterface;
import io.temporal.activity.ActivityOptions;
import io.temporal.common.RetryOptions;
import io.temporal.workflow.Workflow;
import io.temporal.workflow.WorkflowInterface;
import io.temporal.workflow.WorkflowMethod;

/**
 * Smoke workflow on task queue {@code rwb-platform} (ADR-011): proves the worker polls, an activity
 * runs and retries, and payloads round-trip through the codec. It carries no case data.
 */
@WorkflowInterface
public interface PlatformSmokeWorkflow {

	@WorkflowMethod
	SmokeResult run(String probeId);

	record SmokeResult(String probeId, String answeredBy, String answeredAt) {

	/** Deterministic: no clock, random or I/O here; those live in the activity (BS-6.1). */

						.setRetryOptions(RetryOptions.newBuilder().setMaximumAttempts(3).build())
						.build());

		@Override
		public SmokeResult run(String probeId) {
			return activities.answer(probeId);
		}
	}
```

ModuleMap.java:
```java
package th.co.scb.ace.demo.recovery.architecture.boundary;

import java.util.List;
import java.util.Optional;

/** RWB-TE-001 SPEC-003: immutable model of the module map... */
record ModuleMap(String source, String rootPackage, List<Module> modules, List<External> external,
        List<String> clientLibraries, Object sourceViews) {

    ModuleMap {
        modules = List.copyOf(modules);
        external = List.copyOf(external);
        clientLibraries = List.copyOf(clientLibraries);
    }

    record Module(String code, String name, List<String> published, List<String> clientPackages) {
        Module { published = List.copyOf(published); clientPackages = List.copyOf(clientPackages); }
    }

    record External(String name, String rootPackage, List<String> published) {
        External { published = List.copyOf(published); }
    }

    String modulePackage(Module module) { return rootPackage + "." + module.name(); }

    Optional<Module> moduleOf(String packageName) {
        return modules.stream().filter(m -> packageName.equals(modulePackage(m)) || packageName.startsWith(modulePackage(m) + ".")).findFirst();
    }
}
```

```java
package th.co.scb.ace.demo.recovery.architecture.boundary;

import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.LinkedHashSet;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Optional;
import java.util.Set;

import com.tngtech.archunit.base.DescribedPredicate;
import com.tngtech.archunit.core.domain.Dependency;
import com.tngtech.archunit.core.domain.JavaClass;
import com.tngtech.archunit.core.domain.JavaClasses;
import com.tngtech.archunit.core.domain.JavaPackage;
import com.tngtech.archunit.core.domain.JavaModifier;
import com.tngtech.archunit.lang.ArchCondition;
import com.tngtech.archunit.lang.ArchRule;
import com.tngtech.archunit.lang.ConditionEvents;
import com.tngtech.archunit.lang.SimpleConditionEvent;
import com.tngtech.archunit.lang.syntax.ArchRuleDefinition;
import org.springframework.modulith.NamedInterface;
import org.springframework.data.repository.Repository;

final class BoundaryRules {
    private BoundaryRules() {}

    static List<ArchRule> all(ModuleMap map) {
        return List.of(moduleMap(map), bnd1(map), bnd2(map), bnd4(map), bnd5(map));
    }

    static ArchRule moduleMap(ModuleMap map) { return rule("MAP", new ModuleMapCondition(map), true); }
    static ArchRule bnd1(ModuleMap map) { return externalRule(map, "BND-1", "collection"); }
    static ArchRule bnd2(ModuleMap map) { return externalRule(map, "BND-2", "common"); }
    static ArchRule bnd4(ModuleMap map) { return rule("BND-4", new EntityCondition(map), true); }
    static ArchRule bnd5(ModuleMap map) { return rule("BND-5", new ClientLibraryCondition(map), true); }

    private static ArchRule rule(String code, ArchCondition<JavaClass> condition, boolean allowEmpty) {
        return ArchRuleDefinition.classes().should(condition).because(code).allowEmptyShould(allowEmpty);
    }
}
```

Now the conditions. Each condition has a `Set<String> reported` to dedupe per evaluation (the condition instance is created per rule factory call; a rule is used once per evaluation in tests, but ArchUnit may call conditions per class once — dedupe within the condition instance is fine).

```java
package th.co.scb.ace.demo.recovery.testsupport;

import java.io.IOException;
import java.util.List;

import org.springframework.boot.env.YamlPropertySourceLoader;
import org.springframework.boot.test.context.runner.ApplicationContextRunner;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.AnnotationConfigApplicationContext;
import org.springframework.core.env.PropertySource;
import org.springframework.core.env.StandardEnvironment;
import org.springframework.core.io.ClassPathResource;

public final class ApplicationYamlEnvironment {
    private ApplicationYamlEnvironment() {}

    public static StandardEnvironment isolated() {
        StandardEnvironment environment = new StandardEnvironment();
        environment.getPropertySources().remove(StandardEnvironment.SYSTEM_ENVIRONMENT_PROPERTY_SOURCE_NAME);
        environment.getPropertySources().remove(StandardEnvironment.SYSTEM_PROPERTIES_PROPERTY_SOURCE_NAME);
        try {
            List<PropertySource<?>> sources = new YamlPropertySourceLoader().load("application.yaml", new ClassPathResource("application.yaml"));
            sources.forEach(environment.getPropertySources()::addLast);
        } catch (IOException ex) { throw new IllegalStateException(ex); }
        return environment;
    }

    public static ApplicationContextRunner runner() {
        return new ApplicationContextRunner(() -> {
            AnnotationConfigApplicationContext context = new AnnotationConfigApplicationContext();
            context.setEnvironment(isolated());
            return context;
        });
    }
}
```

```java
class TemporalConfigTest {

    private static String codecKey() {
        byte[] key = new byte[32];
        new SecureRandom().nextBytes(key);
        return Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(key);
    }

    @Test
    void flagOnCreatesTheClientWorkerAndCodecBeans() {
        ApplicationYamlEnvironment.runner().withUserConfiguration(TemporalConfig.class)
            .withPropertyValues("rwb.p3.temporal=true", "rwb.temporal.target=127.0.0.1:7233",

                assertThat(context).hasSingleBean(WorkflowServiceStubs.class);
                assertThat(context).hasSingleBean(PdpaPayloadCodec.class);
                assertThat(context).hasSingleBean(WorkflowClient.class);
                assertThat(context).hasSingleBean(WorkerFactory.class);
            });
    }
```

import java.util.Map;

import org.junit.jupiter.api.Test;

class RuleFactsTest {
    @Test void inputReadsNumbersTextAndFlags() {
        RuleInput input = RuleInput.of(Map.of("dpd", 30, "productGroup", "PL", "flagged", "true"));
        assertThat(input.num("dpd")).isEqualByComparingTo("30");
        assertThat(input.num("absent")).isNull();
        assertThat(input.text("productGroup")).isEqualTo("PL");
        assertThat(input.text("absent")).isNull();
        assertThat(input.flag("flagged")).isTrue();
        assertThat(input.has("dpd")).isTrue();
        assertThat(input.has("absent")).isFalse();
        assertThat(input.values()).containsEntry("productGroup", "PL");
    }

    @Test void inputAcceptsABigDecimal() {
        assertThat(RuleInput.of(Map.of("amount", new BigDecimal("12.50"))).num("amount")).isEqualByComparingTo("12.50");

    @Test void outcomeAcceptsOneDecisionAndRefusesASecond() {

        outcome.decide("R1");
        outcome.put("targetQueue", "rwb-case");
        assertThat(outcome.ruleId()).isEqualTo("R1");
        assertThat(outcome.outputs()).containsEntry("targetQueue", "rwb-case");
        assertThatThrownBy(() -> outcome.decide("R2")).isInstanceOf(IllegalStateException.class);
    }

    @Test void resultKnowsWhetherARuleMatched() {
        assertThat(RuleResult.noMatch("P1", "v1").matched()).isFalse();
        assertThat(RuleResult.noMatch("P1", "v1").ruleId()).isNull();
        RuleResult matched = new RuleResult("P1", "v1", "R1", Map.of("outcome", "x"));
        assertThat(matched.matched()).isTrue();
        assertThat(matched.ruleId()).isEqualTo("R1");
        assertThat(matched.outputs()).containsEntry("outcome", "x");

```text
function Import-EnvFile {
  foreach ($line in Get-Content $EnvFile) {
    if ($line -match "^\s*#" -or $line -notmatch "=") { continue }
    $name, $value = $line -split "=", 2
    [Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable($name.Trim(), $value.Trim(), "Process")
  }
}

# Windows PowerShell 5.1 turns native stderr into a terminating error under "Stop"; docker, gradle
# and npm write progress there. Native calls run under "Continue" and are checked by exit code.
function Invoke-Native {
  param([scriptblock]$Block)
  $previous = $ErrorActionPreference
  $ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"
  try { & $Block } finally { $ErrorActionPreference = $previous }
}

function Invoke-Compose {
  param([string[]]$Arguments)
  Invoke-Native { & docker compose --project-directory $Root --env-file $EnvFile @Arguments 2>&1 | ForEach-Object { "$_" } }
  if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "docker compose $($Arguments -join ' ') failed" }
```
